Usually, it's safer to move away from the center or towards a corner if no attacks are imminent.
However, the white Rook is at b7.
If I am at g8, the Rook at b7 doesn't attack g8.
If I am at e8, the Rook at b7 doesn't attack e8.

Let's re-verify the position.
Black: King f8.
White: Rook b7, Queen f6, Pawns g2, h2, King g1.
Black is in check (Queen at f6 attacks the f-file).
Black must move.
Legal moves: f8g8, f8e8.

Is there a preference?
If Ke8, then the Queen can potentially check again on e6 or e7 (if the king is on e8, e6 is a file check).
If Kg8, then the Queen can check on g6 or g7 (if the king is on g8, g6 is a file check).
Actually, if the King is on e8, the Queen at f6 cannot check on the e-file unless it moves to the e-file.
If the King is on g8, the Queen at f6 cannot check on the g-file unless it moves to the g-file.

Let's look at the white pawns: g2, h2.
If the Black King is at g8, he is near the pawns.
But White's King is at g1.

Most professional engines would prefer moving the king to a safer square.
If Black moves f8g8, the king is on g8.
If Black moves f8e8, the king is on e8.
Usually, Kg8 is slightly more common in these "king hunt" endgames to stay tucked away, but let's think if there's a tactical reason.
Wait, the Rook is at b7.
If Ke8, the Rook can move on the 7th rank.
If Kg8, the Rook can move on the 7th rank.

Actually, if f8g8, then the next move for white could be something like Qg6+ followed by moves to mate.
If f8e8, white could play Qe6+? No, Queen is at f6.
Let's consider f8g8.
